# The open issues of Macaulay2/M2

Generated by `bin/render-issues`.  Do not edit -- edit `issues.tsv`.

791 open issues, oldest first.  See [README.md](README.md) for what each verdict means and for why nothing here closes an issue on its own.

## Where it stands

- `todo            ` Not yet triaged                                 431  `###############.............`
- `reproduces      ` Still broken -- the reported failure came back  162  `######......................`
- `stands          ` Still open -- read, and the ask is still unmet  186  `#######.....................`
- `stale-repro     ` Reproducer needs rewriting before it says anything    2  `............................`
- `not-reproducible` No longer reproduces, cause unknown               3  `............................`
- `fixed           ` Fixed                                             3  `............................`
- `not-a-bug       ` Expected behavior                                 3  `............................`
- `needs-reporter  ` Waiting on the reporter                           1  `............................`

### Reproducer runs

`bin/extract-repros` pulls a script out of the issue body and `bin/run-issue-repros` runs it against a clean M2.  **This is a hint, not a verdict.**  Most of these issues report a wrong answer rather than a crash, so a run that completes says nothing on its own; what says something is whether the diagnostics or the values the reporter recorded came back.

- `n/a             ` nothing runnable in the body                          256
- `(none)          ` not run                                               138
- `syntax          ` the *extractor* failed; says nothing about the issue   99
- `differs         ` some value changed -- read it                          98
- `different-error ` failed, but not the recorded way                       40
- `drift           ` failed on API drift                                    38
- `reproduces      ` the recorded diagnostic came back                      36
- `clean           ` ran to completion, nothing recorded came back          35
- `same-output     ` every recorded value is unchanged                      33
- `crash           ` signal or internal error                               11
- `timeout         ` still running at the limit                              4
- `n/a-platform    ` this machine is not the platform in question            2
- `clean-partial   ` an `end` line cut it short; almost nothing              1

### Labels

603 of 791 carry at least one label; 188 carry none.

| [#1012](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1012) | 2019-09-19 | differs | Core | reproduces, verified on 1.26.06-40: trim (coker matrix {{x,x^2,0},{0,0,1}}) over QQ[x] gives subquotient(\| 1 \|, \| 0 x \|; \| 0 \|, \| 1 0 \|) -- 1 generator but 2 relations -- while the P in the body, same generator with the single relation \| x ; 0 \|, is isomorphic to it (inducedMap(N,P) still isIsomorphism). The other 63 sets total ~99KB, 42 of them under 1KB, median 531 bytes. | [#1146](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1146) | 2020-05-13 | different-error | Core,threads | reproduces on current M2; Doug's call is NO COMMENT, so this note carries it. Ran Dan's own second reproducer (task returning 333 alongside 'while true do 1', cancel the loop, check both) for 301 iterations, three times. Synchronized IO (default): 63 anomalies / 10 clusters / first at 37 / gaps 26,30,27,28,27,28,26,27,28; and 55 / 9 / 28 / 30,30,31,31,25,31,32,32. Both symptoms Dan named fire: 'u finished' on a task running an infinite loop, and taskResult t giving null where it returned 333. Anomalies are CLUSTERED, 3-4 consecutive iterations then ~30 clean, in every run -- so short trials see nothing (first anomaly at 28-38), which is why it has always looked random; Dan's published excerpts cover iterations 0-9 only. The period is NOT constant between runs (~27 vs ~30), so 'clustered' is the claim, not a fixed period. TESTED AND REJECTED, Doug's hypothesis that this is an artifact of the default synchronized IO mode: setIOUnSynchronized() (verified to move getIOThreadMode from 1 to 0) gives 54 anomalies / 9 clusters / first at 38 / gaps 30,32,30,30,32,29,31,32 -- statistically indistinguishable. THE FINDING WORTH KEEPING: the 1.25 changelog records 'The taskResult function now waits until a task is finished before returning its result', which IS Dan's diagnosis in the thread ('if you try to cancel a task that has finished, you are still responsible for getting its result') implemented -- and taskResult still returns null in ~11% of iterations, so the leading explanation on the thread is ruled out. Also untouched: Mike's ask for cancellation without an error and backtrace, and Dan's proposed silentInterruptions, which appears nowhere in the tree |

| [#1183](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1183) | 2020-05-21 | differs | Core | reproduces; Doug's call is NO COMMENT. map(GF(16)[y], GF(4)[x], {y}) still sends GF(4)'s generator to GF(16)'s: it echoes as map(S,R,{y, a}), which is what misled Dan into his first reply before he corrected himself. CORRECTION TO MY OWN FRAMING, from Doug: I called it damning that the result is not a ring homomorphism, but map() returns non-homomorphisms routinely and by design -- his example is map(ZZ,QQ), which constructs fine as map(ZZ,QQ,{}) and then errors on g(1/2). So 'not a homomorphism' is not the finding. What is worth recording is narrower and sharper. (1) isWellDefined f returns TRUE for this map, and the reason is that the check is vacuous here: isWellDefined RingMap (m2/newring.m2:259) does flattenRing(source, Result=>3) and tests g(ideal S)==0, but flattenRing(GF(4)[x]) returns the ring UNCHANGED with ideal S = ideal(), so the Galois relation a^2+a+1 never enters the ideal being tested -- meaning the check passes for any map out of a polynomial ring over a GaloisField. That is arguably a separate defect from this issue's subject and may be worth filing on its own. (2) Dan's hint checks out concretely: map(GF(16),GF(4)) alone gives map(L,K,{a^2+a}) and (a^2+a)^2+(a^2+a)+1 = 0, so ConwayPolynomials already computes the correct embedding and the polynomial-ring map simply does not delegate to it. Related: [#1011](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1011) (docs should say ring maps are not enforced well-defined) and [#3961](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/3961) (isWellDefined for ring maps). zeromath offered to fix it 2020-05-23, asked two build questions, Dan answered, silence since |

| [#1424](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1424) | 2020-07-22 | same-output | Core | reproduces verbatim, and it is the SAME defect I found incidentally on [#1183](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1183) last batch, in a different ring class. isWellDefined phi returns true for AviSteiner's map, while [dy,y] = 1 in W and [phi(dy),phi(y)] = 0 in W', so phi does not preserve the Weyl relation. Mechanism, identical to the Galois-field case: isWellDefined RingMap (m2/newring.m2:259-265) does flattenRing(source, Result=>3) and tests g(ideal S)==0, and flattenRing(W) returns W itself with ideal S = ideal() -- the commutator relations live in the multiplication, not in an ideal of an ambient polynomial ring, so the check is vacuous. Generalisation worth stating: isWellDefined RingMap only checks relations expressible as an ideal of the flattened ambient ring, and returns true for any ring whose defining relations live elsewhere. On [#1183](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1183) I noted that vacuity 'may be worth filing on its own' -- it did not need filing; [#1424](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1424) IS that issue, and is the better home for it. Related: [#1011](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1011) (docs should say ring maps are not enforced well-defined), [#3961](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/3961) |

NEW, and different from what it reports: with the task CANCELLED before exit, the default synchronized mode survives 4 threads printing for 10 seconds (5892 lines, exit 0), while the identical script under setIOUnSynchronized() SIGSEGVs after 1679 lines with interleaved output ('hi th-- SIGSEGV'). That isolates the 2024 change -- [`2b141a9f7f`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/2b141a9f7f) / [`471d93a74e`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/471d93a74e) (Dave Barton, 2024-02-23, 'Don't setIOUnSynchronized() since it's not thread-safe', PR [#3186](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/3186)) commented out the setIOUnSynchronized() call at m2/last.m2:17, whose own comment read 'try to avoid deadlocks when running examples'. So one crash mode was removed by stopping M2 from disabling synchronized I/O, and a second remains on exit with a live printing task. threads label already present |

| [#1880](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1880) | 2021-01-26 | differs | Documentation | reproduces, and the mechanism is located. htmlDirectory is still "" when the examples run. installPackage.m2:34 initialises it to the empty string; installPackage.m2:837 calls installHTML, which sets it at line 459 to replace("PKG", pkgname, installLayout#"packagehtml"); but generateExampleResults is called at line 784, fifty-three lines earlier. So every link an example emits is relativized against "" -- and relativizeFilename("", "common/share/doc/Macaulay2/Complexes/html/x.html") returns its argument unchanged, where relativizeFilename("common/share/doc/Macaulay2/Text/html/", same) gives ../../Complexes/html/x.html. Both checked in a live session. The leaked string is the raw build-tree layout tail from htmlFilename (installPackage.m2:99), which in the build layout begins with common/. That also explains pzinn s contrast: in an ordinary session htmlDirectory is "" too, but getPackageInfo resolves an installed package to a different prefix, so toURL(String,String) takes the prefix\|tail branch and yields a correct absolute URL -- html TO "freeResolution" gives href="/usr/share/doc/Macaulay2/Complexes/html/_free__Resolution.html" on 1.26.06-40-gd8e86d689d. Scope in the shipped docs is one file, Text/example-output/___Mark__Up__Type.out, from the example html oo; in Standard mode it reaches the page escaped, so a reader sees a wrong path as literal text rather than a dead link, which is why pzinn said it mostly affects the web app, where the same output is rendered. The ${prefix}$ placeholder he remembers is not findable anywhere in this repository history, and nothing post-processes .out files for links; it may have lived in Macaulay2Web. |

So the four-way inconsistency Doug tabulated -- Example strips, CannedExample strips leading-whitespace comments, EXAMPLE lines refuses a comment-only line, EXAMPLE PRE strips nothing -- is intact, and so is Dan's explanation for the third case, that EXAMPLE lines wants one complete input expression per string. Nobody acted on Mike's 'I'd be happy with leaving comments in the examples'. | [#1917](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1917) | 2021-02-05 | clean | Core,Documentation | Reproduces exactly, and one line explains both halves -- but the overwrite is deliberate, not an oversight. galois.m2:173-178: 'if d >= opts.SizeLimit or primitiveElement != S_0 then (typ = "FlintBig"; primitiveElement = S_0; -- Possibly NOT the primitive element in this case!!'. In the reported ring d = 48 < SizeLimit = 10000, so the trigger is the second disjunct, which fires whenever the primitive element is not the ring's own variable, discarding both FindOne's search (galois.m2:156-161) and the user's argument before galois.m2:200 stores the result as F.PrimitiveElement. Control: in ZZ/7[z]/(z^2-z-3) isPrimitive z is false (order 24 of 48) and F.PrimitiveElement comes back z; in ZZ/7[w]/(w^2-w+3) isPrimitive w is true and F.PrimitiveElement is correctly w; the rings differ in nothing else. Scope of the damage, measured: the substitution exists so ringmap.m2:122/133/142 can tell the engine where S_0 goes rather than where a primitive element goes, and it works -- 200 random pairs through map(F[t], F) on both the FlintBig and Zech paths gave zero additive or multiplicative failures. So arithmetic and ring maps are sound and only the option and the exposed attribute are wrong. The documentation demonstrates the broken usage: ov_rings.m2:644-648 promises 'your own choice of primitive element', says elements are stored internally as powers of it, and shows GF(ZZ/2[T]/(T^9+T+1), PrimitiveElement => T^3+1) -- T^3+1 has order 511, T does not, 511 < SizeLimit, and k.PrimitiveElement returns T. Related but distinct: [#4582](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/4582) on GF's modulus search, [#1993](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1993) on memoizing GF, [#1183](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1183) on ring maps over Galois fields. |
| [#1925](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1925) | 2021-02-09 | differs | Engine | Reproduces, still nondeterministically. Mechanism: degree_in_box (e/free-modules/freemod.cpp:391) loops i < len with len = D->n_vars(), the degree rank of the ring, reading lo->array[i] and hi->array[i] with no check on lo->len or hi->len beyond '!= 0'; select_by_degrees passes ndegrees as len and the glue at d/interface.dd:1877 validates only that the arguments are sequences of small integers. So a degree vector shorter than the degree rank reads past the end of the array. CORRECTION to an earlier draft of this note, which called it a memory-safety bug reachable from the prompt: it is not reachable without debug Core. The one caller in Core, submatrixByDegrees, validates lengths itself at matrix.m2:499-506 and errors 'expected degree vector of length 3' -- verified, submatrixByDegrees(m,{1},{1}) errors while submatrixByDegrees(m,{1,0,0},{1,0,0}) works. So the contract is enforced one layer above the engine and the raw entry point trusts its caller. Confirmed by experiment: degreeLength SM is 3, calls with both arguments length 3 or both empty are deterministic and correct, only the length-1 arguments vary run to run -- which means the report has it backwards, {1} is the malformed argument, not {1,0,0}. mahrud's own follow-up asking for partial-degree support is the well-defined version of the input that currently reads garbage, so one change would settle both halves. Not a duplicate of [#3578](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/3578), which passes correct-length degrees and fails on torsion in the degree group; mahrud links the two as 'Related' himself. |

Root causes 2 and 3, both package-level, were never touched: SuperLinearAlgebra.m2:50 still builds a length-2n weight vector for an n-variable ring, so the Weights block is truncated to n zeros and the intended weights are discarded, and check(2)/check(3) still nest superRing over R rather than QQ. The reference build hides this: three 'no-check-flag [#1984](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1984)' markers at lines 225, 263 and 313 of the installed SuperLinearAlgebra.m2 are Debian/PPA patches, not upstream, so check here skips the failing tests. Mike said on 2026-05-21 that he would take a look. |

| [#2004](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2004) | 2021-03-25 | same-output | Engine | Reproduces exactly: kernel f = ideal() while f(u-v) = 0, on F = frac(QQ[u]), R1 = F[], R2 = F[v], f = map(R1,R2,{u}). Root cause was diagnosed in-thread by jieaosong in 2021 and never acted on: kernel uses selectInSubring(1, ...) on the graph ring (m2/ringmap.m2), but with no variables in R1 the Eliminate 0 block is indistinguishable from the default order, because empty blocks are elided in the engine at e/interface/monomial-ordering.cpp by 'return (p->nvars > 0);' -- so selectInSubring counts blocks wrongly and eliminates the wrong one. Dan asked for empty blocks to stop being treated as absent; Mike offered on 2021-03-27 to fix it if told what was wanted, jieaosong answered (make selectInSubring remember the empty-block information), and nothing followed. IMPORTANT, and about this project rather than the issue: [#4621](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/4621), which we filed on 2026-08-11 from bugs/mike/0-empty-monomial-ordering-blocks, is this same root cause -- Dan's own note quoting the same 'return (p->nvars > 0);' line and the patch attempt that broke many tests. It duplicates this row, and this row was findable when we filed it: gh search issues returns [#2004](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2004) second for 'Eliminate 0', second for 'nvars > 0' and fifth for 'selectInSubring'. It was passed over on its title, 'Incorrect kernel computation', which names a symptom and says nothing about monomial orderings, with the diagnosis sitting in the comments -- the near-miss-title failure again. Also ours and related: [#4618](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/4618), graphIdeal and graphRing accept MonomialOrder and silently ignore it. |

The caching mahrud calls easily avoidable exists but only for one overload -- newring.m2:201 wraps flattenRing GaloisField in cacheValue (symbol flattenRing => opts), while flattenRing Ring at :195 does not -- so the general case still mints a new ring per call, and the documentation's warning that multiple calls may define multiple rings regarded as different objects is still accurate. | [#2154](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2154) | 2021-06-24 | n/a | Core,Linear Algebra | Reproduces, and the number is worse than 'slow': measured on the reporter's own attachment (rankbug.txt, a 32x32 matrix over ZZ/2 in 5 variables), rank transpose M takes 0.0026 s and rank M takes 69.4 s. Both return 32. That is a factor of 27000 for the same mathematical quantity, and the control is exact rather than approximate, because rank is transpose-invariant and the transpose is the only difference between the two calls -- so this is not a performance wish but a degenerate case in one orientation. kschwede measured 56 s against 0.0025 s in 2021, so nothing has improved in four years. His observation that transposing sometimes helps enormously, with no way to predict which matrices, is the substance of the row and is unaddressed; his det timings (0.85 s dense, 1.97 s with Strategy => Cofactor) show the determinant of the same matrix is cheap, which makes a 69 s rank harder to excuse. matthias314's question, 'Is this a bug?', has never been answered, and his second attachment was 692 s against 76 ms in Sage. mahrud's guess in the thread, that Sage may use Flint where M2 uses fflas_ffpack, was never confirmed by Mike or anyone else. |
| [#2159](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2159) | 2021-06-25 | differs | Core,NormalToricVarieties | Reproduces exactly on both halves. Over a local ring the Betti tally loses its total degrees: the global kk[u,v] case gives keys (0,{0},0), (1,{2},2), (2,{3},3) while the localization at (u,v) gives (0,{0},0), (1,{2},0), (2,{3},0) -- the third slot, the weight, is 0 throughout -- and heft of the local ring is null, which is mahrud's diagnosis. Passing Weights => {1,1} still fixes the display. ggsmith's answer is the substantive obstacle and it is not a defect claim: for grading groups of rank above one there is no widely agreed weight vector, and computing a good default, such as a minimal ample line bundle on a smooth toric variety, can be prohibitive when the Picard rank is large. So the local-ring half looks fixable -- heft null is a missing default, not a mathematical open question -- while the Cox ring half is genuinely unsettled. Related and newer: [#2327](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2327) on betti with degree 0 variables, [#2303](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2303) on BettiTally consistency under nonstandard gradings. |
| [#2162](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2162) | 2021-06-27 | syntax | Gröbner bases,arm,build issue,stale | Reproduces, and it is the row the reference build is patched hardest against: no-check markers naming [#2162](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2162) appear in three installed packages -- CharacteristicClasses.m2, IntegralClosure.m2 and SegreClasses.m2 -- so example generation and check here both skip the affected work. Live as of 2025-06-20, when Doug posted an s390x build log with three EngineTests/GB.Test.LinearAlgebra failures: two 'out of memory trying to allocate 32 bytes, exiting' on groebnerBasis(..., Strategy => "F4") and one assertion failure where gbB == gbC is false, i.e. F4 returning a different basis rather than merely dying. The 2021 arc is worth keeping straight: Doug traced it to big-endianness via [Macaulay2/mathicgb#3](https://github.com/Macaulay2/mathicgb/issues/3), found that the current patch in mathicgb#9 did NOT fix it while an earlier version of that patch ([`d8ae074c7f`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/d8ae074c7f)) was partially effective, and each fix moved the failure to a different example -- randomKRationalPoint, then integralClosure(Ideal,RingElement,ZZ), then char(SparseResultant) with std::bad_alloc. Also, older Ubuntu releases passed where newer ones failed on the same example, which points at the toolchain as well as the architecture. LABEL ASKED AND ANSWERED: 'stale' was applied by mahrud on 2024-05-02 and contradicted by Doug's own 2025-06-20 log thirteen months later, so removal was proposed; Doug chose to keep it, on the reading that in this tracker 'stale' means nobody is working on it rather than no recent activity -- which is true, since the standing remedy is to skip the affected tests in packaging. 'Gröbner bases' added. |
| [#2175](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2175) | 2021-07-03 | differs | Core | Reproduces, and both participants are right about different things, which is why it never resolved. The value is commutative and the class is not: intersect(monomialIdeal(x*y^2), ideal(x^2*y)) has class MonomialIdeal, intersect(ideal(x^2*y), monomialIdeal(x*y^2)) has class Ideal, and the two are == as ideals. mahrud's position is that preserving the first argument's type is intended and speeds up algorithms in Saturation; Dan's is that an intersection operator whose result type depends on argument order is not commutative. Neither is contradicted by the other. Related: [#1589](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1589) on removing MonomialIdeal from the top level, which would dissolve the question, and [#4500](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/4500), ours, on monomialIdeal {} error messages. |

The thread reached a working answer rather than a fix -- of the three alternatives mahrud offered, -DREADLINE_ROOT_DIR worked and CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH and CMAKE_SYSTEM_PREFIX_PATH did not -- and mahrud said he would favour simplifying if someone tested it on a Mac, which haerski could not do. So the row is blocked on a Mac test, not on a decision. mahrud also suggested switching to libedit, [#825](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/825), as the better use of the effort. |

The thread is a genuine disagreement that ends in partial agreement worth recording: ggsmith's position is that for a singular variety Pic and Cl differ, the basis comes from a kernel of a ZZ-module map, and the signs carry no meaning -- he twice asked what a 'reasonable' basis would mean and asked for a reference, which mahrud supplied from Cox-Little-Schenck. But ggsmith's last message concedes something specific: the difference arises from the 'fromPic := matrix fromPicToCl X' line in (nefGenerators, NormalToricVariety), the nef cone is normally regarded as lying in the Picard group rather than the class group as the package's own documentation says, and 'I do regard this as a bug'. mahrud replied that he had not changed fromPicToCl and that his only change to nefGenerators, in [`1cdeae8cf50`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/1cdeae8cf50), was to cache it. So there is maintainer agreement that the re-embedding is wrong, and nobody acted on it. mahrud's last lead, unfollowed, is that the divergence starts in cartierDivisorGroup where fromCDivToWDiv is cached. |

Dan located it precisely: the illegal == is not in the vertex-set construction fchapoton suspected but in the edge comprehension, 'if (u_0 == v_0 and member(set {u_1, v_1}, edges H)) or (u_1 == v_1 and ...)'. He also offered a simplification, V := vertexSet G ** vertexSet H in place of toList(set ... ** set ...), and an aside that is arguably its own issue: it is strange that vertexSet returns a list rather than a set. | [#2264](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2264) | 2021-10-23 | crash | Engine | Reproduces exactly and still aborts the process: 'R = ZZ/3[x,y,z]; isSupportedInZeroLocus(ideal vars R, HH_1 koszul vars R)' gives 'terminate called after throwing an instance of std::logic_error, what(): ERROR: Inserted duplicate entry into a KD tree' and dumps core on 1.26.06. As with [#1984](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1984), this is a C++ exception escaping to std::terminate rather than being turned into a recoverable M2 error, so the interpreter never gets a chance to report it -- the same class of defect one library over, here in the KD tree used by the mathic/frobby side rather than in Monoid. mahrud's own observation that pruning the homology first avoids it is the workaround and also the clue: the duplicate entry presumably comes from unpruned generators that coincide. |
| [#2269](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2269) | 2021-10-27 | drift | Linear Algebra | Reproduces exactly. With D = makeWeylAlgebra QQ[x_11,x_12,x_21,x_22], a = x_11*dx_21 + x_12*dx_22 and b = x_22*dx_22 + 2, a*b ends in 3*x_12*dx_22 while (matrix{{a}}*matrix{{b}})_(0,0) ends in 2*x_12*dx_22 -- so matrix multiplication is still performed in the opposite algebra. What makes this row unusual for this corpus is that all four maintainers who spoke agreed it should change: Dan called it unnatural and said he and Mike had discussed fixing it, then 'I'm in favor of fixing it, since it's surprising'; Mike agreed but noted it is a larger change because it must stay compatible with Groebner bases; moorewf gave the argument in full -- since M2 already uses right modules and so represents linear maps as left multiplication, the multiplication should not be happening in the opposite ring -- and said he could not remember the reason for the current implementation. Against that, Anton told the reporter on Math StackExchange that it is intentional and the entries are to be read as coming from the opposite algebra. So the disagreement is between the thread and an off-tracker answer, and nothing was done. moorewf's second point is also untouched: the Kronecker product of matrices does not represent a tensor product of maps here in the way it would commutatively. |
| [#2284](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2284) | 2021-11-05 | n/a | Engine,Gröbner bases | Cannot be observed on the reference build, and the reason matters more than the row. The assertion jkyang92 hit is MATHICGB_ASSERT(false) at e/mathicgb/mathicgb/F4MatrixBuilder2.cpp:133, in the else branch of a three-way dispatch over tasks[i].sPairPoly. That macro is live only under MATHICGB_DEBUG: stdinc.h:135 defines it as assert(X) inside '#ifdef MATHICGB_DEBUG' and :139 defines it as MATHICGB_ASSUME(X) otherwise -- which on GCC and Clang is ((void)0) at stdinc.h:82. (The __assume(X) form at :19, which would make this undefined behaviour, is the MSVC branch and does not apply.) So in a release build the check is a plain no-op: control falls out of the else branch, tasks[i] keeps both poly and sPairPoly set, and the state the author considered impossible is left in place rather than reported. Ran the attached reproducer (files/[`7480862`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/7480862)/test2.m2.txt) five times on 1.26.06: all five completed cleanly, which is evidence about the assertion being compiled out, NOT about the bug being gone. jkyang92 saw it 'most of the time' from a source tree with DEBUG defined. Two connections: [#1206](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1206), still open, is exactly this gating -- M2's configure stops defining NDEBUG under --enable-debug while mathicgb wants DEBUG defined -- so nobody gets these assertions by default; and [#2162](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2162), where F4 'sometimes gives wrong results' on s390x, is the shape a suppressed check of this kind would produce. jkyang92 suspected a race or memory corruption and noted he is on amd64, unlike [#2162](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2162). |

Zero comments in four years. Same territory as [#2159](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2159), triaged in batch 39 and also reproducing, where a null heft over a local ring zeroes the weights entirely. |

| [#2372](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2372) | 2022-01-17 | n/a | Documentation | Reproduces, and the open question our own comment posed now has an answer. The comment posted 2026-08-06 under the marker bug-triage:bugs/dan/1-doc-headlines asked how linSpace acquired a hypertext headline given that document requires a string at document.m2:601-604 and SimpleDoc enforces the same through singleString at SimpleDoc.m2:81. The source is Polyhedra/documentation/documentation.m2:102-103, which has 'Headline' followed by 'Deprecated version of @TO "linealitySpace"@'. So singleString cannot catch it: @TO "..."@ IS a single string at parse time and only becomes a Hypertext object when the doc string is processed, which means the check is at the wrong level rather than being bypassed. Offered as a reading rather than a proof -- I did not trace the processing. The design question the comment raised stands unanswered: this issue asks for non-strings in headlines to error, while bugs/dan/1-doc-headlines asks for headlines to support everything including TeX, and the asymmetry is that allowing markup obliges every consumer of headlines to render it, including terminal help, about results, info files and package tables of contents. |

Verified: HH^1(X, OO_X(-2,2)) is QQ^6 and Ext^1(OO_X, OO_X(-2,2)) is QQ^0 on X = toricProjectiveSpace 1 ** toricProjectiveSpace 2, so they disagree where Hartshorne III 6.3 says they must agree, and mahrud's belief that QQ^6 is right is consistent with HH being the one computed correctly. In 2022 ggsmith got 'expected sheaves on a projective variety' and reasonably asked whether mahrud had altered his local copy. What changed: the sheaf code moved out of Core into the Varieties package, and Ext(ZZ, CoherentSheaf, CoherentSheaf) now lives at Varieties.m2:758-765 with NO projective-variety guard -- that check survives only in Varieties/SheafComplexes.m2 at :159, :186 and :210. So the input that used to be refused is now accepted and answered wrongly, which is a regression in safety rather than in arithmetic. ggsmith's substantive point is untouched and explains the wrong answer: the algorithm assumes sheaves represented by ZZ-graded modules over a standard graded polynomial ring, and the implementation computes via basis(0, E) on G(>=0), which is exactly that assumption. | [#2658](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2658) | 2022-11-03 | drift | package issue | Reproduces byte-for-byte, all three outputs, 4 years and 0 comments on.  Identified the mechanism.  projectiveBundle' is correct: its pi_*OO(1) equals chern V exactly, for E (rank 2), G (rank 3) and Sym2 E -- the Grothendieck answer pi_*O(1) = V.  projectiveBundle is off by a det twist: pi_*OO(1) === chern(dual V ** det V) === chern exteriorPower(rank V - 1, V) in every case, never chern dual V, which is the classical/Fulton answer the reporter (correctly) expected.  Cause: tautologicalLineBundle FlagBundle (Schubert2.m2:781-785) sets OO(1) = OO_X(sum(1..#B-1, i -> i*chern(1,B#i))), and projectiveBundle V is flagBundle({1, rank V - 1}, V), so B#1 is the rank-(n-1) QUOTIENT and OO(1) becomes det Q = det V ** dual S rather than dual S.  Pushing that forward gives det V ** dual V.  Verified the reporter's hand computation is right: for rank-2 E with roots a,b, Sym2 E has roots 2a, a+b, 2b, giving 1+3c1+(2c1^2+4c2)+4c1c2, matching M2's chern symmetricPower(2,E).  Same area as [#330](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/330), which is about these two conventions being undocumented. |
| [#2718](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2718) | 2022-12-25 | clean | Engine,Linear Algebra | Reproduces, and the cause is now pinned. Measured on 1.26.06 with the reporter's own 120x120 matrix (det A = 1, so genuinely unimodular): A^-1 over ZZ ran 151 s and died at 3.9 GB under a 6 GB cap, while (transpose A)^-1 took 0.018 s, promote(A, ZZ[])^-1 0.019 s and promote(A, QQ)^-1 0.014 s -- all three of pzinn's contrasts confirmed, a ~1000x asymmetry on the same data. Cause: inverse Matrix (matrix1.m2:623-630) routes to basicInverse only when hasEngineLinearAlgebra (ring m); that is FALSE for ZZ and true for QQ, RR_53, CC_53 and ZZ/32003, so ZZ falls to quotientRemainder(id_(target m), m), a division computation whose cost depends on pivot structure -- which is exactly why the upper-triangular transpose is instant. Second layer: even forced onto the engine path, rawLinAlgInverse on a dense mutable ZZ matrix raises "'invert' not implemented for this kind of matrix over this ring", pzinn's error. Reason: inverse(const DMatZZGMP&) at mat-linalg.hpp:659 is a stub that throws, while a working implementation sits 111 lines below at :770 -- inverse(const DMatZZ&) using FLINT's fmpz_mat_inv, which even rejects non-unit denominators, precisely what a unimodular matrix needs. So the fast code exists in the same file and is not wired up. Answers pzinn's 2024 question to mikestillman, which got no reply. |
| [#2741](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2741) | 2023-01-09 | same-output | Engine | Reproduces on every ring tried, confirming mahrud's own conclusion that it is broken altogether: f mutableMatrix{{x}} gives 0 over toField(QQ[i]/(i^2+1)), CC, ZZ/11 and QQ, while the immutable path is correct (over ZZ/11, h z = z^2 and h matrix{{z}} = matrix{{z^2}}). Cause found: rawRingMapEvalMutableMatrix at e/interface/ringmap.cpp:70-83 is a STUB -- it returns MutableMatrix::zero_matrix of the right shape and carries the comment '// TODO: now map it!'. So it silently returns correctly-shaped zeros rather than erroring. Introduced by mikestillman in [`cc2940453d`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/cc2940453d), 2014-06-14, titled 'Adding promote, lift, ring map eval for mutable matrices, part 1' -- part 2 never came, so this has returned zeros for over 12 years. The (SPACE, RingMap, MutableMatrix) method does exist (ringmap.m2:189), so dispatch is fine; it is the engine call underneath that does nothing. |
| [#2779](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2779) | 2023-02-16 | differs | package issue | Reproduces: methods frobenius is still empty on 1.26.06, and the code mahrud linked (TestIdeals/frobeniusPowers.m2:38-42) has had no commits since the report. Mechanism confirmed: the four methods are all registered on frobeniusMethod -- methods frobeniusMethod gives (frobeniusMethod,ZZ,Ideal), (frobeniusMethod,ZZ,Matrix), (frobeniusMethod,Ideal), (frobeniusMethod,Matrix) -- while frobenius is a distinct object, 'new FrobeniusOperator from frobeniusMethod', so a lookup keyed on frobenius finds nothing (frobenius === frobeniusMethod is false). Note methodOptions frobenius DOES work and returns the option table, so it is specifically methods that fails. Workaround for users: debug TestIdeals, then methods frobeniusMethod. |

Core/quotring.m2:292-298 is unchanged since 2007: singularLocus Ring takes minors of the jacobian of the presentation with no commutativity test, and singularLocus Ideal just forwards to it. | [#2810](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2810) | 2023-04-12 | differs | Engine | Reproduces on 1.26.06-40. basis(2,E) for E = QQ[e_0..e_3, SkewCommutative=>true] is e_0e_1 e_0e_2 e_0e_3 e_1e_2 e_1e_3 e_2e_3, while the six columns of (koszul vars QQ[x_0..x_3]).dd_2 read, in the same notation, e_0e_1 e_0e_2 e_1e_2 e_0e_3 e_1e_3 e_2e_3 -- the third and fourth are transposed, which is the reporter's e_0^e_3 < e_1^e_2 point, verbatim. |
| [#2820](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2820) | 2023-05-01 | differs | Engine | Reproduces, and the defect is two levels below prune. prune M still gives coker\|1 0;0 1;0 0\| where kk^1 is right -- but prune of THAT gives kk^1, so prune is not idempotent here. The hook that fires is minimalPresentation's 'isAffineRing R and isHomogeneous M' one (Core/modules2.m2:167-174; M is trivially homogeneous because degreeLength kk = 0), returning coker modulo(complement f, f). complement takes the isField branch (m2/matrix2.m2:170-172), h = transpose syz transpose f, and syz is where it goes wrong: for f = matrix{{a^2+1,a},{a,a^2},{1,a+1}} over kk = toField(QQ[a]/(a^3-a-1)), transpose f has rank 2 so its syzygy module is a kk-line, but syz transpose f returns a 3x3 whose columns are c, a*c, a^2*c -- the QQ[a]-module generators of that line, i.e. syzygies computed over the underlying polynomial ring rather than over the field. complement then hands back a 3x3 with two zero columns instead of a 3x1, and modulo yields two redundant generators. With constant entries syz transpose matrix{{1,2},{3,4},{5,6}} returns the correct 3x1, which is why the second prune succeeds. |
| [#2822](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2822) | 2023-05-09 | clean | package issue | Reproduces exactly: filtration P = {{0},{1,2},{3}}, filtration dual P = {{3},{1,2},{0}}, filtration dP = {{2,3},{1},{0}}. The line is Posets.m2:441 inside dual Poset -- 'if P.cache.?filtration then Q.cache.filtration = reverse P.cache.filtration' -- under posets'Precompute, which defaults to true (Posets.m2:51), and reversing a filtration gives the dual's filtration only when P is graded, exactly as the reporter says. Sharper tell than the reporter had: the same block copies minimalElements and maximalElements across correctly (lines 451-452), so one Poset object contradicts itself -- filtration Q starts {{3}} while minimalElements Q is {2,3}. |
| [#2826](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2826) | 2023-05-13 | reproduces | Core,Linear Algebra | Reproduces, and the recursion is not in the determinant at all: precision A for a FreeAlgebra loops forever between 'precision Ring := R -> precision 0_R' (m2/reals.m2:29) and 'precision RingElement := precision @@ ring' (m2/enginering.m2:15), and getMinorsStrategy tests 'precision R < infinity' first (m2/multilin.m2:31). minors(2,m) hits the same loop. Name a strategy and it computes: exteriorPower(2,m,Strategy=>Cofactor) is -cb+da and exteriorPower(2,m,Strategy=>Bareiss) is ad-cb. They disagree -- which is Mike's 2025 question answered, and the answer was already written inside getMinorsStrategy by its authors: 'Note that if R is non-commutative then either algorithm is incorrect. What is the correct thing to do in this case?' (multilin.m2:27-30). So moorewf's 'just give an error' has a second, cheap half: precision FreeAlgebra should terminate whatever det ends up doing. |

| [#662](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/662) | 2017-07-27 | clean | Core | still open, code byte-identical to 2017 at m2/matrix1.m2:562. Answered mahrud's unanswered 2024 question 'what's an example where it's not well-defined': R=QQ[x,y]/(x^2*y-y^3), ideal(x^2)==ideal(x^2,y^3) but their [3,2] powers are ideal(x^6) vs ideal(x^6,y^6). Mechanism: the result is the extension along phi=map(R,R,{x_i^e_i}), generator-independent iff phi is an endomorphism; over a polynomial ring it always is (76 equal-ideal pairs, 0 failures) but over a quotient x_i->x_i^e_i need not carry the defining ideal into itself and M2 does not check (40 of 2274 cases disagree). Sound on polynomial rings, unsound on quotients -- narrower than the title. Comment lists three remedies. PROPOSED retype Feature->Bug (pre-existing Feature, not mine): equal ideals giving unequal results is a correctness defect, not a missing feature |

| [#984](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/984) | 2019-08-10 | n/a | Engine | stands. Two DIFFERENT measurements, and Doug caught me conflating them: the issue asks both to 'speed up compilation' and 'to only include what is needed', and I first measured only the former. (1) COST. All 202 engine TUs compiled serially with real flags: 80925 src -> [`23919597`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/23919597) preprocessed, 528 s, mean 2.61 s. Control I should have run first: empty TU 947 lines, #include <vector> 23050, vector+iostream+map+memory 58355 -- against which my 'worst ratio' table was artifact (interrupted.cpp 0.67x a normal STL file, ringelem.cpp 1.27x); the ratios were large only because those sources are tiny. What survives: 34 TUs above 4x baseline, clustered within 3% at 258k-347k, i.e. one shared header. Chain: aring-glue.hpp 287106 -> aring-translate.hpp 272745 -> aring-ZZp-ffpack.hpp 258118 and aring-tower.hpp 258282 (costly only via ffpack); all 14 sibling aring-* headers are ~80000. Root is '#include <fflas-ffpack/ffpack/ffpack.h>', 1320 ffpack header inclusions. This is a cost finding, NOT an IWYU finding -- that class exposes ffpack types in its interface, so it may well be including what it needs. (2) HYGIENE, the issue's actual ask, measured with iwyu 8.21 over all 202 TUs: 397 unneeded includes across 144 files, 1406 missing across 186, only 11 files completely clean. Worst are basic-mutable-matrices/mat.cpp (17 remove/12 add) and dmat.cpp (11/31) -- exactly the 'RingType templates for dense and sparse matrices' the body names, and also in the cost cluster. (3) BUT IWYU'S ADVICE DOES NOT APPLY MECHANICALLY HERE. Applying its 15 removals to a copy of mat.cpp gave 288195 -> 101815 preprocessed and 13.90 s -> 0.79 s, which I nearly reported as a 17x speedup: it was a COMPILE FAILURE ('invalid use of incomplete type MutableMat<DMat<ARingZZGMP>>'), since IWYU drops mutablemat.hpp which the file's explicit template instantiations need. Adding its 8 suggested additions too still fails -- it proposes SLP/SLP-imp.hpp, an implementation header that does not compile standalone. That is probably why the 2020-21 passes ([`ddb01c6632`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/ddb01c6632), [`0ee9bfdc0e`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/0ee9bfdc0e), [`aa0b7fa644`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/aa0b7fa644), [`f26df24a8a`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/f26df24a8a), [`bf6fdbc4d2`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/bf6fdbc4d2); 114 files annotated) stopped where they did. No source modified: experiment ran on copies in scratchpad |

So the enabler exists and the package on top of it was never written. mahrud's other complaint still holds: getWWW on an ftp:// URL returns the empty string rather than erroring. | [#1865](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1865) | 2021-01-23 | n/a | Core | stands. The body is a screenshot with no text, so there is no runnable repro; read it and the title makes sense -- '-- making example results for "reading files"' is followed on the same line by 'sh: line 1:  1776 Abort trap: 6', and Dan circled both instances of that. The mechanism is unchanged and deliberate. Four call sites write a padded progress line with no newline so the elapsed time can be appended: m2/examples.m2:250 (with the comment '-- the timing info will appear at the end'), m2/examples.m2:257, m2/testing.m2:46 in checkmsg, and m2/run.m2:159 for the error line. Anything a child process writes to stderr before the timing is appended therefore lands on that line. Not reproduced here rather than reproduced: it needs an example that aborts under installPackage, and at an interactive prompt M2's own prompt separates the two writes. The intended one-line form is visible in ordinary output, e.g. ' -- skipping  check(0, "DeterminantalRepresentations") -- .000083201s elapsed'. |

| [#2183](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2183) | 2021-07-08 | syntax | arm,build issue | Untestable on armhf here, but two things are established. (1) The failing SVD is purely advisory. The message comes from cond_number_via_svd at e/NAG/NAG.cpp:1269, called at :1933 from the path tracker to fill t_s->cond, the condition number of the Jacobian along the homotopy path; that field is written there and read only at :2154 by getSolutionRcond, which hands it to M2 as a solution attribute. It gates no decision in the tracker. Yet on info > 0 the function calls ERROR("zgesvd did not converge") and the whole computation aborts -- a diagnostic that cannot be computed is treated as fatal. The non-fatal pattern is three lines above the call: 'if (!LAPACK_success) t_s->status = SINGULAR;', so the tracker already records LAPACK trouble as a solution status rather than an error. (2) The test passes on amd64: the installed marker is '-- no-check-flag [#2183](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2183)', which captureTestResult honours on every architecture, so it is skipped here too, and extracting the body and running it directly passes. IMPORTANT -- do not read that broad skip as a judgement about scope. Doug explained the history: he skipped everything when he began writing these Debian patches, added the no-check-architecture mechanism later, and never went back to narrow the old ones. Measured: his packaging carries 17 blanket no-check-flag entries against 14 granular ones, with the blanket set skewing old (13 of 17 under [#2400](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2400)) and the granular set carrying the newer numbers. So the marker says when it was written, not where the test fails. Recorded in README.md. Doug's own remark -- never seen on armhf PPA builds or his Raspberry Pi, only the Debian armhf builder -- plus [#2230](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2230), a LAPACK 3.10.0 assertion in check(4) of this same package, still points at the BLAS/LAPACK implementation rather than at armhf as an instruction set; a sweep of test 8 across six LAPACK implementations on amd64 was run to test that and is reported separately. LAPACK SWEEP, at Doug's request: not supported on amd64. Ran test 8's body under six implementations x 20 seeds via LD_PRELOAD -- reference LAPACK 3.12, ATLAS, OpenBLAS serial/pthread/openmp, and MKL -- and all 120 runs passed with zero zgesvd non-convergences. The control that makes this meaningful: LD_DEBUG=bindings confirms M2-binary binds zgesvd_ to the preloaded library (e.g. atlas/liblapack.so.3), so the swapped implementation really was being called; the binding appears once because lazy binding resolves it once and later calls go via the PLT. This is the weak direction, as flagged before running it: it rules out 'any LAPACK will do this anywhere' but leaves armhf's own floating point, or that one Debian armhf builder, possibly combined with whichever LAPACK it links -- none of which is testable here. Which is why the advisory-SVD finding above is the actionable one: recording a status instead of calling ERROR makes the failure non-fatal on every architecture regardless of what stalls zgesvd. |

| [#2222](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2222) | 2021-09-03 | n/a | Gröbner bases | Unmet, in both the general and the specific form. gb.m2:438-446 defines forceGB as a method with only a forceGB Matrix overload, so the forceGB(Matrix,Matrix) that Dan proposed in the Google-group comment MichaelABurr quoted -- asserting that one matrix is a Groebner basis for another -- does not exist; nor does the other half of that comment, that 'gens G' should know G is its own Groebner basis. The underlying ask, feeding partial or degree-limited Groebner information into a later computation, has nothing implementing it. The motivation is concrete and still live: SubalgebraBases computes many nearly identical partial bases and discards the information between them. Closely related and also open: [#686](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/686), kernel of a ring map should remember its Groebner basis, and [#996](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/996), the ideal of a Groebner basis should remember it has one -- all three are the same 'do not throw away what we just computed' complaint from different directions. |

I had written that version#"flint version" supplies the version half of Dan's request and only the documentation fails to surface it. That half is unmet and now cheap to settle, because the call chain is short and fully identified: isPseudoprime ZZ at m2/integers.m2:85 calls rawZZisProbablePrime, which is d/interface.dd:4533 into e/interface/flint.cpp:31-39, which calls fmpz_is_probabprime. The doc says only 'This function calls a function in the FLINT library', naming neither the function nor the algorithm. Evidence for the algorithm, kept separate from inference: /usr/include/flint/fmpz.h:752-754 declares fmpz_is_probabprime_lucas, fmpz_is_probabprime_BPSW and fmpz_is_probabprime together, so BPSW and Lucas are the pieces flint provides -- but which of them the general entry point dispatches to for a given size should be read from flint's own documentation rather than taken from me, since the header only shows they are neighbours. Also worth fixing while someone is in there: the sentence 'there are no known numbers which are composite, and for which this function returns true, although it is expected that there are an infinite number of such primes' (isPrime-doc.m2:27-29) ends on 'such primes' where it means such composites. And the contrast with isPrime is undocumented and worth a line: isPrime goes to fmpz_is_prime, a proof, while this goes to fmpz_is_probabprime. |

| [#2350](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2350) | 2021-12-08 | syntax | Interpreter | Stands. Unmet: there is no top-level 'when ... is ... else' and 'when' is not a keyword at the M2 prompt, though it is one in the d language. ASKED AND ANSWERED: Doug was leaning wontfix and asked for the case; on being shown that this is a general switch-statement request rather than a request to mimic d's when, he said keep it. That distinction is the whole thread and it is easy to miss, because it lives in the fourth of five comments. mahrud's opening invokes d's when AND asks for inheritance-awareness, so Dan read it as type dispatch and replied 'dispatching via method functions is probably enough for all practical purposes'; two comments later Dan says so explicitly -- 'you mentioned inheritance in your original post, and invoked the "when" statement in the D language, so I inferred you intended this to be used for type dispatch' -- and mahrud answers 'No I meant a general switch statement', with an example switching on opts.Strategy, a Symbol rather than a type. So Dan's objection was withdrawn by clarification, not sustained, and his LAST word is a build recipe rather than a refusal: add the keywords with suitable parsing precedence, add an item to the union type Code and one to ParseTree, and follow ifCode and IfThenElse. That recipe is the durable output of the thread and is why the row is worth keeping -- it makes this a specified starter project for someone learning the interpreter, which is the gap [#2247](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2247) is about. Arguments the other way, recorded so they are not re-derived: it is convenience rather than capability, since Core already switches on Strategy symbols twelve times with plain 'if opts.Strategy === Foo then' and type dispatch already supplies the inheritance mahrud wanted; the cost is a language change touching parser, converter and evaluator; and [#1978](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1978) is the same shape of stalled parser-convenience request, which Dan resisted on the ground that it makes the language more complicated. No retitle proposed: the title does say 'switch statements', so it is the body's framing that misleads, not the title. |

Dan's position: a symbol should belong to one package, exportFrom breaks that, M2 then picks up the other package's example code for the symbol and reruns it -- which can fail because not all of that package's symbols are present -- and he floated removing exportFrom altogether. mahrud's position: exportFrom means the symbol is now part of this package, he uses it, and the right fix is a check that it was not already exported elsewhere. Dan's objection to that is concrete and unanswered: verifying no other package exported it would mean loading every package. His own last proposal, also unacted-on, is to add a field to symbol closures recording which package a symbol was exported from, so double export could be prevented cheaply. Greg Smith's failing files, which Dan offered to forward, never reached the thread, so the demonstration mahrud asked for twice is still missing. Nine comments, all from 2022-05-05 and 2022-05-06, nothing since. |

## Reproducer needs rewriting before it says anything -- `stale-repro` (2)

| issue | created | run | labels | note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [#2728](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2728) | 2022-12-30 | drift | Memory Leak | Not reproduced, but under weaker conditions than reported, so keeping it open. Built the current tree with --enable-debug --with-system-libs on GCC 13.3.0 and probed it three ways: a 2000-iteration registerFinalizer stress, 40 GB computations over QQ[a..e], and a 300-iteration Set - Set loop, each followed by collectGarbage. Zero 'GC Warning: Finalization cycle' messages, in both that debug build and the release 1.26.06. It is genuinely a debug build -- the run emits the debug-only '--finalization: (n)[addr]: obj' traces, so finalizers are running and being logged. What I could NOT run is mahrud's actual trigger, building the Macaulay2Doc documentation in debug mode: my build was partial (c, e, d, system, bin, m2 only) so no documentation is installed and capture examples raises. A full debug doc build is hours and was out of scope for this row. Separately, the thread is stalled on an unanswered question rather than on a reproduction: mahrud asked Dan on 2023-01-02 for the exact gdb commands to break on the registration of one specific finalizer pointer, and got no reply in three and a half years. Related: [#1515](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1515), which mahrud cross-referenced, and [#2774](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2774) in this same batch, another finalizer-timing crash. |
| [#2774](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2774) | 2023-02-10 | different-error | Engine | Not reproducible here, but the report is credible and intermittent so keeping it open. Ran Doug's 2024 reproduction (subring sagbi eqns over QQ[x_1..x_9, MonomialOrder => Lex]) six times on 1.26.06: 6/6 completed cleanly, no SIGSEGV. Consistent with the thread -- mahrud could not reproduce it in 2023 either, Doug saw it once in a 2023 PPA build and once in a 2024 CI run. Both traces are a finalizer libgc invoked DURING an allocation: gbvector_remove reached from ReducedGB::~ReducedGB via libgc in 2023, and from gbA::remove_gb via finalize.cpp:108, GC_invoke_finalizers and GC_generic_malloc_many inside newClass (actors2.dd:238) in 2024. Source detail worth keeping: the crash site is not just list-walking -- gbvector_remove_term ends in mem->delete_elem(f), and mem is 'stash *mem', a member of the GBRing (gbring.hpp:84). So the crash is consistent with the GBRing or its stash being gone when the GB's finalizer runs. Unresolved and deliberately NOT published (Doug's call, no comment): GB objects register with GC_REGISTER_FINALIZER_IGNORE_SELF (finalize.cpp:115,117) while monideals, resolutions, SchreyerOrders and MutableMatrices use the plain GC_REGISTER_FINALIZER. That may be deliberate, since GB objects are self-referential and would otherwise never be collected, but it weakens libgc's ordering guarantee -- gc.h documents that plain registration orders finalization ('if a and b are inaccessible, and a points to b, then only a will be finalized') and that true cycles are never finalized at all, which is the leak Dan describes on [#2728](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2728) rather than unordered finalization. Could not determine whether the ignore_self choice is the path to this crash. Related finalizer rows: [#2728](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2728), [#1515](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1515). |

| [#1903](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1903) | 2021-01-31 | crash | Engine,arm,build issue | The reference build is patched against this row: /usr/share/Macaulay2/AssociativeAlgebras/tests.m2 carries '-- no-check-flag [#1903](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1903)' inside TEST blocks 29, 30 and 33, and captureTestResult skips on that marker on every architecture, so a clean check here would prove nothing. Ran the three bodies directly instead: all pass, 3x each, under jkyang92's 2022 amd64 reproducer (a schedule'd collectGarbage loop -- which needs allowableThreads raised or the thread is interrupted at once; 4140 collections observed concurrent with test 30), and test 30 also passes under GC_FREE_SPACE_DIVISOR=1000 and GC_MAXIMUM_HEAP_SIZE=400M. So 'I can reliably reproduce this crash on amd64' no longer holds on this machine. Not proposed for closing: it is a GC race, passing runs do not prove it gone, and the armhf original is untestable here. Engine added because the whole stack trace is e/NCAlgebras + poly.cpp + aring-glue. arm was proposed for removal -- it stopped being arm-specific within days, via Doug's 20.04 x86-64 CI link of 2021-02-01 and jkyang92's amd64 reproduction in 2022 -- and Doug kept it: armhf is where his buildds see it, the three no-check-flag skips exist for arm builds, and an arm sweep should still find this row. |
| [#2025](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2025) | 2021-04-07 | differs | Interpreter | The behaviour reported here -- the first QQ["x_1"] erroring and an identical second call succeeding -- is gone. Both calls now fail identically, with 'expected strings, integers, or symbols' rather than 'baseName: no base name available'. This row already carries a comment from this project posted 2026-08-08 under the marker bug-triage:bugs/dan/1-variables, which records that plus the wider ask from that bugs/ file: of QQ["x"], QQ["x,y"], QQ["x_1"] and QQ["x_1 .. x_4"], only the bare name works, and the replace(String,ZZ,String,String) and replace(String,ZZ,Function,String) overloads that file thought were needed first do not exist. So no further comment is proposed. mahrud's 2021 diagnosis still holds -- getSymbol was not written for indexed variables, and getSymbol "x_0" installs the literal string "x_0" in the private dictionary, distinct from the IndexedVariable x_0. |
| [#2511](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2511) | 2022-05-20 | differs | Core | Split outcome, so it stays open. The reported defect is gone: the issue's claim is that 'A == reducedRowEchelonForm A' is true for A = matrix{{0_(ZZ/2),0,0},{1,0,1},{0,1,1}}, and it is now FALSE -- rref returns matrix {{1,0,1},{0,1,1},{0,0,0}} with the zero row at the bottom. Deliberately NOT called fixed, because I could not name the cause: rowReducedEchelonForm in the engine has no change since 2018, rawLinAlgRREF none since 2020, and the fflas_ffpack submodule pointer last moved in 2021-12, before this was filed. The reference build links the system fflas-ffpack rather than the submodule, so the likeliest explanation is a distro library version, which would leave no M2 commit to cite. The second half of the ask is unmet and is why the row is kept: reducedRowEchelonForm still supports few rings -- QQ works, ZZ errors (fair, not a field), and a toField output errors, which is exactly the case jchen419 named along with cyclotomicField. Related, ours: [#4598](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/4598) on pivots assuming column echelon form. |

No longer hangs -- errors at Core/gb.m2:160 per the guard agreed in this very thread |

Doug's call: RETITLE and keep open rather than close, because the live content is now mahrud's 2019 offer to generalize solve via syzygies -- 'I can make a PR to do that if you'd like' -- which Dan put to Mike and which was never answered, and which dylanpeifer restated in 2020, also unanswered. Scope has genuinely moved: the title 'Bug in solve' describes a wrong answer that can no longer occur. verdict fixed refers to the defect; action is comment, not close |
| [#1928](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1928) | 2021-02-09 | syntax | Interpreter | Fixed by the second of the two options mahrud offered ('add a breakpoint keyword that continue will not get stuck on ... I really don't care which one'). The breakpoint keyword was added by mahrud himself in [`59ea23120c`](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/commit/59ea23120c) (2024-08-24) and shipped in PR [#3431](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/3431) 'top-level profiling' (merged 2025-02-09, first released in 1.25.05) without mentioning this issue; interp.dd:774 handles continueMessage with eval(c) rather than re-entering the debugger. Verified against 1.26.06: 'breakpoint print "..."' stops before the expression, and one continue runs it and leaves the debugger, so f 12 returns 12 -- the output mahrud said he expected. Caveats for the close: error-as-a-breakpoint is unchanged and still loops on continue; the help text at m2/code.m2:261 still describes continue the old way; and the debugger prints a spurious 'unhandled continue command' line before doing the right thing. |

## Expected behavior -- `not-a-bug` (3)

| issue | created | run | labels | note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [#330](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/330) | 2015-08-13 | syntax | Documentation | not a defect: reproduces byte-identically, and jmokland's 2015 remark is confirmed -- segre F equals the pushforward under projectiveBundle' (quotients) but not projectiveBundle (subs). Each convention is documented on its own page; the interaction is not, and segre's own dual is provisional per its doc. Kept open as a doc task |
| [#1958](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/1958) | 2021-02-24 | same-output | Core,Engine | The reported difference is deliberate on both sides, and the reason QQ[x] differs from the other three rings is not the one given in the thread. gcd(QQ,QQ) never reaches the engine at all: m2/integers.m2:51-53 defines it as a rational content gcd -- clear both denominators to a common d, take the integer gcd of the numerators, divide back -- so gcd(2_QQ,4_QQ) is 2. ZZ[x] keeps its content because ZZ is not a field. Only QQ[x] goes through rawGCDRingElement, where the answer is normalized over the base field, and 2 and 8 are units there, so the gcd is 1. Mike's in-thread answer ('over a base field it always gives a monic value') explains QQ[x] but not why QQ itself behaves like ZZ, which is exactly what mahrud asked. Verified: {ZZ,QQ,ZZ[x],QQ[x]} -> {2,2,2,1}, gcd(2_QQ,8_QQ)=2 and gcd(2_(QQ[x]),8_(QQ[x]))=1. Two residues are unmet: the gcd documentation (operators.m2:208-235) still says only 'the greatest common divisor of the arguments' with nothing about normalization, which is the documentation mahrud asked for on 2021-02-26; and the LocalRings behaviour that motivated the whole issue is unchanged -- liftUp matrix{{1/2*y},{1/2*y}} still gives 2y in both rows. Related but distinct, and newer: [#4583](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/4583), [#4622](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/4622). |
| [#2562](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2562) | 2022-08-15 | clean | Memory Leak | Measured, and it is bounded growth rather than a leak. Running f = () -> (scan(0..10^7, i -> ());) repeatedly with collectGarbage between: RSS goes 124,788 kB at baseline, then 1,090,940 after the first call, 1,650,364 after the second, 1,655,464 after the third and 1,655,464 after the fourth -- so it grows for two calls and then plateaus. That matches Mike's report ('uses consistently 1.37 GB') and Dan's explanation: the value of a top-level expression is retained even with a trailing semicolon, each element carries type and gmp overhead, and libgc tracks every allocation, so 10^7 elements is on the order of a gigabyte. mahrud's counter -- that the value is one object -- is answered by the fact that the object is a sequence of 10^7 pointers. His transcript showing 6.4 GB to 8.5 GB used over three calls is consistent with the first-two-calls growth above and he stopped there. So the row is not a leak; what is real is the cost of materialising 0..10^7 at all, which is [#2561](https://github.com/Macaulay2/M2/issues/2561), kept open. Left open rather than proposed for closing since the verdict is mine and the reporter's underlying complaint has a live home. |
